¶ **ON LONG LIFE.**
For those who labor extensively with the **spirit** <term>spirit: a subtle, vaporous substance in the body that connects the physical organs to the soul, often exhausted by deep study or intense thought</term>. Accordingly, the selection of pure and luminous air, odors, and music pertains to no one more than to them. For these three are judged to be the primary **nourishments** <note>original: "fomenta" — literally "kindling" or "fuel," used here to mean things that sustain or stimulate the spirit</note> of the **animal spirit** <term>animal spirit: the highest form of spirit, refined in the brain and responsible for thought, sensation, and movement</term>. But the most powerful of these for life is chosen air. For many born in the eighth month survive in Egypt, and some do so in the temperate regions of Greece by the benefit of the most healthy air—a fact which **Aristotle** <note>Aristotle (384–322 BC): Greek philosopher whose works on biology and physics formed the basis of medieval science.</note> records and **Avicenna** <note>Avicenna (Ibn Sina, c. 980–1037): A Persian polymath and one of the most significant physicians of the Islamic Golden Age.</note> confirms. 

¶ But indeed, just as the body is composed of various elements and must be nourished by various foods—though not all at the same table—so the spirit, being similarly composite, must be delighted and fostered by a certain variety of always-chosen air. It must also be daily refreshed by a similar variety of choice odors. For air and odor seem to be, as it were, certain spirits themselves. 

¶ Now indeed Alexander and Nicolaus the **Peripatetics** <note>Peripatetics: Followers of Aristotle’s philosophy; specifically referring here to Alexander of Aphrodisias and Nicolaus of Damascus.</note>, together with **Galen**, conclude that the **vital spirit** <term>vital spirit: the spirit refined in the heart that provides life and heat to the body via the arteries</term> and the animal spirit are nourished both by odor and by air. This is because both are mixed and conformable to the spirit, and both, once inhaled, penetrate into the **precordia** <term>precordia: the region of the body around the heart and diaphragm, considered the seat of vital heat</term>. There, they are "cooked" and tempered for the service of life, and diffused through the arteries. Once both have been "cooked" again, they nourish both types of spirit (as they say), but especially the animal spirit. 

¶ They also say that breathed air is beneficial not only for cooling the internal heat, but also for nourishment. For even very cold animals breathe. They add that thicker air...

The spirit is indeed the first thing to live within us, and it lives most intensely; it is as if the spirit alone truly lives. Is it not the case that often, due to some sudden accident or strong emotion, life, sensation, and movement suddenly desert the limbs? This happens because the spirit has retreated to the innermost chambers of the heart. Frequently, these functions return to the limbs immediately through massage <note>original: "frictiones" — literally "rubbing," used as a medical treatment to stimulate circulation</note> and odors, as the spirit returns to those parts. It is as if life itself dwells in the spirit—being a volatile thing—rather than in the thick fluids <term>humors: the four bodily fluids (blood, phlegm, yellow bile, black bile) believed to control health and temperament</term> or the physical limbs. # ON THE LONG LIFE

Increase your blood with nutrients that increase it—namely, blood that is tempered and clear. Cherish this blood always with choice air. Nourish it daily with sweet odors. Delight it with sounds and songs. But meanwhile, beware of odors that are too hot, and flee from those that are too cold. Seek out those that are tempered <note>In the medical tradition of this time, "tempered" means perfectly balanced between the four qualities of hot, cold, moist, and dry.</note>. Balance the cold with the hot, and the dry with the moist. Know indeed that every odor, because it is the most subtle part of a body, possesses some degree of heat; and expect that odors will provide nourishment primarily from those things that are themselves nutritious: such as from the aromatic pear, the peach, and similar fruits. But [expect this] even more from fresh, warm bread; most of all from roasted meats; and especially from wine. And just as a flavor that is wonderfully pleasing is the cause or occasion of abundant and rapid nutrition for the body, so you should consider that odors behave toward the spirit <term>spirit: a "spiritus" or vaporous substance that flows through the nerves and arteries, connecting the soul to the body</term>.

I am pleased to remind you again: Democritus <note>Democritus (c. 460–370 BCE) was a Greek philosopher. This story about him delaying his death to avoid spoiling a festival for his family was a popular anecdote in Renaissance literature on aging.</note>, when he was on the very verge of death, in order to comply with the wishes of his friends, maintained his spirit for up to four days simply by the smell of warm bread; he would have preserved his spirit even longer if he had wished. There are also those who say he did this with the scent of honey. For my part, if he did use honey, I believe he poured honey dissolved in white wine over warm bread; for indeed, the scent of honey is not to be despised. For honey is the "flower of flowers," and it provides no small amount of nourishment by its very sweetness and long-lasting quality...

...I would even say it was sugar, if it took on the scent. Cinnamon is similar; as are leopard’s bane <note>original: "doronicum," a plant in the sunflower family often used in medieval tonics</note>, anise, and sweet fennel <note>original: "marathrum"</note>, if they added a greater sweetness to their own sharpness. But what temperament nature has not created, you must create for yourselves. And whenever you fear the scattering of the spirits <term>spirits: the "spiritus" or vital vapors thought to sustain life and movement</term>, apply these scents to hotter, sharper, and most subtle things, which are able to restrain the flight of the spirit for a while and make it stay: such as saffron, cloves, cinnamon, toasted bread, rose water, and rose vinegar, rose, myrtle, violet, sandalwood, coriander, quince, and citrus.

I dread camphor, however, when one must act against graying hair <note>In the humoral theory of the time, camphor was considered "cold" in the extreme; coldness was associated with aging and the whitening of hair.</note>. But I always love fresh mint; it is healthy for the mind <note>The author is likely making a play on words here: the Latin "menta" (mint) sounds like "mens/menti" (mind).</note> and safest for the spirit. Finally, remember that all things contrary to poison are very healthy for life—not only by taste, but also by smell—especially theriac <term>theriac: a famous ancient "universal antidote" made of dozens of ingredients, including opium and viper flesh</term>. We have described these in the book against the plague, and we will describe them again in the following book. 

Among these, lest anything be hidden from you, we also include wine. For just as hemlock is a poison to man, so wine is a poison to hemlock <note>Historical medical texts often claimed that wine acted as a specific antidote to hemlock poisoning if administered correctly.</note>—not if drunk at the same time, but shortly after. And so that I do not entice you here with scents alone, I command you to compose an electuary <term>electuary: a medicinal paste or conserve made by mixing powdered herbs with honey or syrup</term> to be tasted every morning, sweet in smell and taste, and very healthy for life. 

Take three ounces of chebulic myrobalans, one of emblic, one of Indian, and one of belliric <note>These are different varieties of the Myrobalan plum, a staple of both Ayurvedic and medieval European medicine, prized for their purgative and restorative properties.</note>; and a half-ounce indeed...

<meta>This page continues the discussion of life extension, shifting from the preparation of the "Medicine of the Magi" to the astrological cycles that govern human aging. # ON LONG LIFE.

In the same manner that Saturn ruled the infant lying hidden in the womb in the first month, and the Moon in the last; so, immediately after birth, with the order now reversed, let the Moon lead in the first year of life. In the second, if you wish, Mercury. In the third, Venus. In the fourth, the Sun. In the fifth, Mars. In the sixth, Jupiter. In the seventh, however, Saturn. And thereafter, a similar order is repeated throughout the course of life.

Therefore, in every seventh year of life, a very great change occurs in the body. For that reason, it is most dangerous, since Saturn is commonly a stranger <note>original: "peregrinus"; in astrology, a planet is "peregrine" when it has no essential dignity in its current position, often making its influence unpredictable or weakening.</note> to us; and from him, who is the highest of the planets, the governance returns immediately to the Moon, the lowest of the planets. Greek astronomers call these years *climacterics* <term>climacterics: critical points in life, believed by ancients to occur every 7 or 9 years, where the body undergoes significant transformation or risk</term>. We call them *scalar* or *stepped* years; or by necessity, we call them *decisory* <note>original: "decretorios"; years that "decide" the fate of one's health.</note>. 

Perhaps, indeed, in illnesses, the planets rule the humor itself or the nature of one's condition through the days in the same order. Whence every seventh day is called "judiciary" <note>original: "iudiciarius"; also known as a "critical day" in medieval medicine, when a disease was expected to reach a turning point or "crisis."</note> for the same reason. The fourth day is also notable, because it holds the middle position in the seven-day sequence. 

You, therefore, if you desire to extend your life to old age, uninterrupted by such "steps" or sudden falls: whenever you approach any seventh year, consult an astrologer diligently. Learn from what direction danger threatens you. Then, either go to a physician, or summon your own prudence and temperance. For even Ptolemy <note>Claudius Ptolemy (c. 100–170 AD), the most influential astronomer and astrologer of antiquity.</note> confesses that the threats of the stars can be held back by these remedies. He adds, moreover, that the [influence] of the stars...

->h.iii.<-

<meta>This page continues the argument that the human lifespan is not a fixed, unalterable fate. ...[life] having been granted can be so increased, just as a tiller of the fields increases the power of the earth. Peter of Abano <note>Petrus Aponensis (1250–1316) was an influential Italian physician and astrologer who sought to reconcile scientific observation with spiritual and astrological influences.</note> proves with many arguments, and by the testimony of Aristotle, Galen, and Haly <note>original: "Aly"; likely referring to the Arab astrologer Haly Abenragel or the physician Haly Abbas, both highly regarded in the Middle Ages.</note>, that the natural end of life is not determined to a hair’s breadth <note>original: "ad unguem"; a Latin idiom literally meaning "to the fingernail," signifying something polished or measured with extreme precision.</note> from the beginning, but can be moved further or closer. He asserts this both from the influence of the stars and also from the condition of matter. He concludes from these authors and reasons that even a natural death can be deferred: both by the engines of astrology and the protections of physicians. 

Therefore, we do not labor in these precepts rashly. Neither should you be loath to inquire of physicians what your natural diet should be, and of astrologers, which star favors your life. And when this star is well-disposed, and the moon is moving toward it, compose those things which you have learned to be beneficial. Nor should you be ashamed to often listen to those who seem to have attained a prosperous old age not so much by luck as by virtue <note>original: "virtute"; here implying a combination of moral discipline, physical regimen, and skillful living.</note>. 

Furthermore, Ptolemy and other professors of astronomy promise a prosperous and long life by certain images <term>images: astrological talismans or amulets engraved with specific symbols and made of metals or stones corresponding to the planets, intended to "capture" celestial influence.</term> made from specific stones and metals, fabricated under a certain star. Truly, concerning images

# CONCERNING LONG LIFE

...we have composed. Regarding that celestial favor I mentioned just now for the sake of a long youth—now, speaking as much as a poet is allowed to speak, and acting as much as a physician is allowed to act—we shall seek this favor from Phoebus <note>Phoebus is a common epithet for Apollo, the god of the sun, light, music, and healing.</note> and Bacchus <note>Bacchus, or Dionysus, is the god of wine and frenzy. In this context, he represents the vital heat and "spirit" found in the fruits of the earth.</note>.

The youth of the Sun is eternal through Phoebus and Bacchus.
¶ For it is fitting that both gods have unshorn hair <note>original: "intonis crinis." In classical tradition, long, flowing hair was a primary symbol of perpetual youth and vigor.</note>.

Phoebus and Bacchus are always inseparable brothers; both are almost the same being. Phoebus is indeed the very soul of that sphere <note>referring to the celestial sphere of the Sun</note>. The sphere itself is Bacchus. Or rather, Phoebus is the entire circuit of the sphere, while Bacchus is that fiery circle within this circuit. Or better yet: Phoebus is the nourishing light within this fiery globe, and Bacchus exists there as the health-giving heat produced by that light. Therefore, they are always brothers and companions, almost always "one and the same." 

And why is this? If the Sun in springtime is indeed Phoebus, rousing the songs of birds with his own melody and tempering the seasons with his lyre <note>original: "cithara." The author uses the lyre as a metaphor for how the Sun "tunes" the climate and the rhythms of life.</note>, then in autumn, the same Sun exists as Bacchus, the creator of wine. 

¶ That father Liber <note>Liber Pater was an ancient Italian god of fertility and wine, long identified with Bacchus.</note>, who loves the hills, brings three things to us for the preservation of youth. First, these very hills, bathed in sun. Second, on these hills, a most particularly sweet wine. Third, an everlasting sense of security found within wine. Three things also Phoebus, the brother of Bacchus, bestows with equal kindness: Daily [light]...

Almost all poets sing of the three Fates. We too, though we are not poets, sing of three "frugalities" <note>The author is making a play on words between "Parcae" (the Fates) and "parcitas" (frugality or moderation). In the Renaissance, it was a common rhetorical device to link the name of the Fates to the idea of "sparing" or "being moderate."</note>. Indeed, a prudent frugality in all one's sustenance begins a long life for us. Also, a constant frugality in undertaking cares and labors extends life. However, a "frugality" <note>Here used in the sense of a "lack" or "deficiency."</note> that is negligent in enjoying the gifts of heaven cuts life short. Pythagoras <note>An ancient Greek philosopher who emphasized harmony, mathematics, and a disciplined lifestyle.</note> celebrates three temperances above all others; we also celebrate three in this present work:

Keep temperance in your emotions; keep temperance in all your food and drink. Observe the temperament <note>The "temperament" of the air refers to its quality and balance (hot, cold, moist, dry) in the medical theory of the four humors.</note> of the air. For by this foresight, with God’s breathing upon you, you will drive far away that imbalance of the humors which is the cause of rapid old age and untimely death.

That Author of Life <note>A reference to God as the ultimate source of vital breath or "spiritus."</note> will breathe upon you, if you desire a long life only on this condition: that you live longer for the benefit of the human race, and most of all, that you live for Him, through whose breathing the whole world lives.

# ON THE CELESTIAL LIFE.
### Preface to the Book "On the Celestial Life."

**M**ARSILIO Ficino of Florence, to the most serene and ever-invincible King of Hungary <note>original: "Pannoniae Regi". Pannonia was the Roman province that included modern-day Hungary; Ficino is addressing King Matthias Corvinus.</note>.

Most happy King of all, the ancient philosophers most diligently investigated the powers of the celestial bodies and the natures of lower things. Since they believed that a man is wise in vain who is not wise for himself, they seem to have deservedly directed their entire investigation primarily toward attaining for themselves a life from the heavens. For they judged (as I believe) that the elements and the things composed from them would be known to them in vain, and that the movements of the celestial bodies and their influences would have been observed with too much rashness, unless these things—known and joined together at the same time—should eventually lead them to a life of happiness. 

Moreover, such contemplation seems to have benefited them, first of all, in this present life. For Pythagoras <note>A Greek philosopher and mathematician who taught the harmony of the spheres.</note>, Democritus <note>Known as the "laughing philosopher," he was an early proponent of atomic theory.</note>, and Apollonius of Tyana <note>A Neo-Pythagorean philosopher and reputed wonder-worker of the 1st century AD.</note>, and whoever else studied this most intensely, attained prosperous health and a long life through the practical use of the things they had learned. 

It contributed, furthermore, to the future life, both through the spreading of their fame among posterity and through the enjoyment of eternity with God. For indeed, from the wonderful order of the entire world, they finally recognized its Ruler, and loved Him—once known—above all things. To you, however, your magnanimity promises glory for all ages to come...

<summary>This page serves as the preface to Book 3 of Ficino's "Three Books on Life," where he justifies the study of astrology and natural magic by citing ancient philosophers who used such knowledge to achieve longevity and recognize the divine order of the universe.</summary>

# ON THE CELESTIAL LIFE

## **V** The words of Marsilio Ficino to the reader of the following book. <note>original: "Verba Marsilii Ficini Ad Lectorem sequentis libri." Ficino transitions here from addressing his royal patron to addressing you, the reader, directly.</note>

**S**alutations, ingenious guest. Greetings again, whoever you are, eager for health <note>original: "salutis." In Latin, *salus* is a pun; it means both a "greeting" and "health" or "well-being." Ficino is playing on the idea that by greeting the reader, he is also offering them the health they seek.</note>, you who approach our threshold. See, I pray, eager guest, first of all, how hospitable I am. It was certainly the part of the one entering to offer a greeting immediately upon being hosted. But I, anticipating your greeting, commanded you to be well <note>original: "salvere te iussi." Another play on *salus*: "I bid you hello" and "I bid you be healthy."</note> as soon as you were in sight.

I have willingly received you as you enter, though you are still a stranger. If you linger a while with me, I will grant you that promised health (if God breathes His favor upon us). You have therefore found a lodging that is friendly indeed to all, and now full of love toward you. 

If by chance you carry anything with you contrary to love; if you hold any hatred, I pray you lay it aside before you touch these vital medicines <note>Ficino views his philosophical and astrological instructions as "medicines" for the human spirit.</note> here. For love gave life to you, and pleasure gave it entirely. Life, in turn... <note>The text breaks off here at "Vitam uiciss[im]" and will likely continue to discuss how life should be sustained by the same forces that created it.</note>

...brings. If some things perhaps please you less, you may indeed set those aside. But do not, for that reason, reject the rest. Finally, if you do not approve of astronomical images <note>original: "imagines astronomicas." These were astrological talismans or amulets engraved with celestial symbols designed to capture the influence of specific planets to heal the body or spirit.</note>—which were otherwise invented for the health of mortals, and which I myself do not so much endorse as recount—then with my permission, and even if you wish, by my advice, you may pass them over. 

At least do not neglect medicines that have been strengthened by some celestial support, unless by chance you have no concern for your own life. For I have long since found through frequent experience that there is as much difference between medicines of this kind and others made without astrological timing <note>original: "delectu astrologico." This refers to the practice of "electional astrology," or choosing the most auspicious moment (when the planets are favorably aligned) to prepare or administer medicine.</note>, as there is between pure wine <note>original: "merum." In the ancient world, "merum" was undiluted wine, considered far more potent and effective than the common water-mixed variety.</note> and water. 

Indeed, I have seen an infant born in Florence in the month of March, in the eighth month from his conception, at night while Saturn was rising and in retrograde <note>original: "Saturno retrogrado." In astrology, a planet in retrograde (appearing to move backward) is often considered weakened or "malefic." Saturn, the planet of cold and melancholy, rising at birth in this state was thought to be life-threatening for a newborn.</note>—half-dead. Yet through such astrological diligence, he seemed to have been returned to life rather than merely saved by us, or rather by God. And now he has completed nearly three years in good health. 

Now indeed, if I were to relate more stories of the same kind, I would be speaking the truth. Nor shall I present them boastfully (which is entirely foreign to a philosopher), but rather as an encouragement. But we have already spoken enough to you, partly to win your favor and partly to encourage you; hereafter, therefore, let us speak alongside Plotinus <note>Plotinus (c. 204–270 AD) was the founder of Neoplatonism. Ficino's "Third Book" is largely a commentary on Plotinus's ideas about how the soul relates to the stars.</note>, so that we may finally provide for you more diligently.

[blue paragraph mark] In all things which are treated by me here and elsewhere, I wish only so much to be asserted as is approved by the Church. <note>This is a standard "safety clause." Ficino was a priest and his work on "astrological images" bordered on magic, which could be seen as heresy. Here he submits his findings to the judgment of the Catholic Church to avoid conflict with the Inquisition.</note>

<summary>Ficino defends the use of astrological timing in medicine, citing a miraculous recovery of a premature infant, and concludes by submitting his work to the authority of the Church.</summary>
<keywords>astrological images, talismans, electional astrology, Saturn retrograde, Plotinus, medical experience, Church authority, Florence</keywords>

<meta>This page begins the third book of Marsilio Ficino's "Three Books on Life," titled "On Obtaining Life from the Heavens." It follows the preface where Ficino defended the use of astrological medicine. Here, he begins the theoretical foundation of how celestial influences reach the physical world through the "Soul of the World."</meta>

-># THE BOOK OF MARSILIO FICINO THE FLORENTINE<-
-># ON OBTAINING LIFE FROM THE HEAVENS<-
-># COMPOSED BY HIM AMONG HIS OWN<-
-># COMMENTARIES ON<-
-># PLOTINUS<-

->## In what the power that attracts favor from the heavens consists according to Plotinus: namely, that the Soul of the World, the souls of the stars, and the souls of the Daemons <note>original: "Demonumque." In the Neoplatonic tradition Ficino followed, "daemons" are not evil spirits, but intermediary beings or "guardian spirits" that inhabit the air and celestial spheres, acting as messengers between gods and humans.</note> are easily enticed by appropriate forms of bodies. Chapter One.<-

**S**i there were only these two things in the world—Intellect on one side and Body on the other—but the Soul <note>original: "Anima." Ficino is describing the Neoplatonic hierarchy of reality: the static, perfect Intellect (Nous); the mediating, moving Soul (Psyche); and the passive, material Body (Soma).</note> were absent, then the Intellect would not be drawn to the body. For the Intellect is entirely immobile and lacks the "affect" <note>In this context, *affect* refers to a capacity to be moved or to have an impulse toward something.</note> that is the principle of motion, being as far distant from the body as can be. Nor would the body be drawn toward the Intellect, for the body is by itself ineffective and unfit for movement, and is far removed from the Intellect. 

But if the Soul, which is conformed to both, is placed between them, an attraction will easily occur from both sides and toward both. First, the Soul itself is the most easily led of all things, since it is the "primary mover" <term>primum mobile: In ancient physics, this refers to the first thing that moves and thus transmits motion to everything else; it moves by its own nature and will.</term>, and it moves by itself and of its own accord. Furthermore, since the Soul is (as I have said) the intermediary of all things <note>Ficino famously called the Soul the *copula mundi* or "the bond of the world," the "glue" that holds the spiritual and material realms together.</note>, it contains all things within itself in its own way...

<summary>Ficino introduces the "Soul of the World" as the essential intermediary that bridges the gap between the eternal Intellect and the material Body, allowing celestial influences to act upon the physical world.</summary>
<keywords>Soul of the World, Anima Mundi, Plotinus, Neoplatonism, primary mover, Daemons, celestial influence, hierarchy of being</keywords>

--- Page 130 ---
<meta>This page continues Chapter One of Book Three. Ficino is explaining the metaphysical "mechanics" of how the World Soul acts as a bridge between the eternal, unchanging Intellect (the realm of Ideas) and the transient, physical world of matter. He introduces the concept of "seminal reasons" as the specific tools the Soul uses to shape the world.</meta>

and is connected from both sides by a close relationship. Therefore, the Soul is reconciled to all things: even equally to those things which are distant from one another, because they are not distant from the Soul itself. For besides the fact that on one side the Soul is conformed to divine things, and on the other to perishable things, and inclines toward both by its "affect" <note>In Ficinian psychology, "affect" is the Soul's power to be moved or to direct its attention and desire toward either the spiritual or the material.</note>, it remains entirely within itself while being simultaneously everywhere.

Added to this is the fact that the Soul of the World possesses from the divine at least as many "seminal reasons" <term>seminal reasons: (Latin: *rationes seminales*) These are the formative, rational blueprints or "seeds" within the World Soul that translate the static Ideas of the Divine Mind into the physical shapes and powers of living things.</term> of things as there are Ideas in the Divine Mind. By these same reasons, the Soul fashions just as many species in matter. 

¶ Whence each species responds to its own proper Idea through its own proper seminal reason; and through this reason, it can often easily receive something from that Idea, since it was indeed created from that Idea by means of this very reason. Therefore, if a species should ever degenerate from its own proper form, it can be formed anew by this medium which is closest to it <note>i.e., the seminal reason</note>, and through that medium, it can easily be restored to its original state. 

And if you should rightly apply to a certain species of things, or to an individual of that species, many things which are scattered but conform to that same Idea, you will soon draw down a singular gift from that Idea into this matter so opportunely prepared—specifically through the seminal reason of the Soul. For it is not the Intellect itself that is properly led <note>Intellect is too high and immobile to be "drawn" or "summoned" by physical objects.</note>, but the Soul. Therefore, let no one think that by certain materials of the world, certain divinities <term>numina: divine powers or spirits</term> who are entirely segregated from matter are being drawn down. Rather, it is Daemons <note>original: "Demones." In the Neoplatonic tradition, these are not "demons" in the modern sense, but intermediary spirits of the air and stars that act as messengers between the divine and the human.</note> and the gifts of the animate world and of the living stars that are attracted.

¶ Again, let no one wonder that the Soul can be, as it were, enticed through material forms. Since

# ¶ DE VITA CAELITVS.
# ¶ ON THE CELESTIAL LIFE.

...[the Soul] itself creates baits <note>original: *escas*; literally "food" or "baits," here meaning physical objects that attract spiritual forces.</note> of this kind suitable for itself, by which it may be enticed, and it always and willingly dwells in them. Nor is there anything to be found in the entire living world so deformed <note>original: *deforme*; in this context, "without form" or "chaotic" rather than simply "ugly."</note> that the Soul is not present to it, or in which the office <note>original: *munus*; meaning a function, gift, or duty of the Soul.</note> of the Soul is not active. Therefore, Zoroaster <note>The legendary ancient Persian prophet, whom Renaissance thinkers like Ficino regarded as a primary source of "Ancient Theology" (*Prisca Theologia*).</note> called these "congruities" <note>The fitting correspondence between a physical shape and its spiritual blueprint.</note> of physical forms to the reasons of the World Soul "divine lures"; and Synesius <note>A 4th-century Neoplatonist philosopher and bishop who wrote on dreams and providence.</note> confirmed that these were "magical allurements."

¶ Finally, let no one believe that all gifts whatsoever are drawn out from the Soul toward a specific type of matter at a specific time. Rather, depending on the opportunity, only the gifts of the "seed" from which such a species sprouted, or of similar seeds, are drawn. Thus, a man who employs only human things does not claim for himself the specific gifts of fish or birds from that source, but rather human gifts and those similar to them. 

¶ However, when things pertaining to a specific star and its Daemon <term>Daemon: (Greek: *daimōn*) In the Neoplatonic tradition, this is not an evil "demon" but a mediating spirit or protective power that links a person or object to a specific celestial influence or star.</term> are applied, the person undergoes the specific influence <note>original: *influxum*; the "flowing in" of celestial power, which is the literal root of our word "influence."</note> of that star and Daemon, just as wood prepared with sulfur is ignited by a flame that is present everywhere. This influence is received not only through the rays of the star and Daemon themselves, but also through the Soul of the World, which is present everywhere. Within that Soul, the "reason" <note>Referring back to the *rationes seminales* (seminal reasons) discussed previously.</note> of every star and Daemon flourishes: partly as a "seminal reason" for generating things, and partly as an "exemplary reason" for knowing them. For the Soul, according to those even older than the Platonists, built [the world] with its reasons...

<summary>Ficino explains that the World Soul is attracted to physical matter through "magical allurements" or "baits" that match its inner reasons. He argues that by using materials corresponding to specific stars or spirits (Daemons), one can "ignite" and attract specific celestial influences into themselves.</summary>
<keywords>World Soul, Zoroaster, Synesius, seminal reasons, celestial influence, Daemon, Neoplatonism, astral magic, congruity</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of how the World Soul organizes the material world. The previous page ended mid-sentence discussing how gifts are drawn from the stars and their "daemons" (guiding spirits). This page picks up with the action of the Creator or the World Soul establishing the celestial architecture.</meta>

...[established] <note>The word fragment "uit" at the start of the page completes "constituit" (established) from the previous sentence.</note> beyond the stars in the heavens, certain figures <note>original: *figuras*; here referring to the constellations and geometric arrangements of the stars.</note>: and such parts of them that they themselves are also certain figures. He also impressed specific properties upon all of these. Moreover, in these stars—the figures, their parts, and their properties—all the species of inferior things <note>original: *rerum inferiorum*; meaning things on Earth, which are "below" the celestial spheres.</note> are contained, along with their own properties. 

Indeed, he established forty-eight universal figures: namely, twelve in the zodiac and thirty-six outside of it. Likewise, in the zodiac, he set thirty-six according to the number of the "faces" <note>original: *facierum*; in astrology, "faces" (also called decans) are ten-degree subdivisions of a zodiac sign.</note>. Again, in the same place, he set three hundred and sixty according to the number of degrees. For in any given degree, there are many stars from which images <note>original: *imagines*; the technical term for the symbolic forms or constellations associated with specific points in the sky.</note> are constructed. Similarly, he divided the images outside the zodiac into many figures according to the number of their own faces and degrees. 

Finally, he established certain relationships <note>original: *habitudines*; the "habits" or orientations of the stars toward one another.</note> and proportions between these universal images and other universal images, which themselves also exist there. Indeed, figures of this kind each possess their own continuity derived from the rays of their stars, which are cast upon one another by a certain peculiar property. From these most perfectly ordered forms, the forms of inferior things depend, being organized from that celestial source. 

But even those celestial forms, though they are distinct from one another, proceed from the "reasons" <note>original: *rationibus*; these are the "seminal reasons" or divine blueprints within the World Soul.</note> of the Soul which are joined together; and being in a certain way changeable, they proceed from things that are stable. But insofar as these celestial forms do not comprehend themselves, they are referred back to the forms in the Mind... <note>original: *mente*; referring to the Divine Intellect, the level of reality above the World Soul in Neoplatonic philosophy.</note>

<summary>Ficino describes the celestial hierarchy, explaining how the 48 constellations, zodiac decans, and individual degrees serve as universal blueprints that govern every species on Earth. These physical star-patterns are manifestations of stable, divine "reasons" held within the World Soul.</summary>
<keywords>astrology, constellations, zodiac, decans, World Soul, celestial rays, Neoplatonism, seminal reasons, Ptolemaic astronomy</keywords>

# ON LIFE FROM THE HEAVENS

...whether animal or something more eminent, comprehending themselves; these, though manifold, are reduced to the most simple One and Good, just as celestial figures are reduced to the pole. 

But let us return to the Soul. Therefore, when the Soul generates the specific forms and powers of inferior things <note>Meaning things on Earth, which are "below" the celestial spheres.</note>, it effects them through its own <term>seminal reasons</term> <note>original: *rationes seminales*; these are archetypal "blueprints" or "seeds" within the Soul that contain the formative power to create physical things.</note>, with the aid of the stars and celestial forms. 

But the singular gifts of individuals—which are often present in some people and are as wonderful as those found in entire species—it provides similarly through seminal reasons. This occurs not so much with the aid of the celestial forms and figures, but rather by the positions of the stars, the state of their motions, and the <term>aspects</term> <note>original: *aspectuum*; the specific angular relationships (like squares or trines) between planets that astrologers believe determine their influence.</note> of the planets, both among themselves and in relation to the stars higher than the planets. 

Indeed, our soul, beyond the specific powers of its members <note>i.e., the individual organs of the body.</note>, brings forth a common power of life everywhere within us. But it does so especially through the heart, as if from a fountain of fire closest to the soul. Similarly, the World Soul, flourishing everywhere, especially through the sun, unfolds its power of common life in all directions. Hence, some place the soul—both in us and in the world—in every member in its entirety, but most especially in the heart and the sun. 

But always remember that just as the power of our soul is applied to our members through the spirit; so the power of the World Soul, through the <term>fifth essence</term> <note>original: *quintam essentiam*; also known as the ether, this is a celestial substance that mediates between the spiritual soul and the material world.</note>—which flourishes everywhere like a spirit within the body of the world—is expanded through all things under the World Soul. This power is especially infused into those things which contain the most of this kind of spirit...

...drawn. ¶ This **quintessence** <term>quintessence</term> <note>original: *quinta essentia*; the "fifth essence" or aether. In Renaissance thought, this was a celestial substance that permeated all things, acting as the bridge between the material world and the divine soul.</note> can be taken up by us more and more again, if one knows how to separate it to a great degree when it is mixed with other elements; or at least if one frequently uses those things which abound in it, especially in its purer state. 

Such things include choice wine, sugar, balsam, as well as gold, precious stones, **myrobalans** <term>myrobalans</term> <note>original: *mirobalani*; dried astringent fruits used in traditional medicine, believed by Renaissance scholars to have life-preserving properties.</note>, and those things which smell most sweetly and which shine. But this is found most especially in those things which, in their *subtle substance*, possess a quality that is warm, moist, and clear <note>These three qualities—warmth, moisture, and clarity—were considered the essential characteristics of the "Spirit," the vital heat that sustains human life.</note>. 

Besides wine, such a thing is the whitest sugar; particularly if you add to it gold, and the fragrance of cinnamon and of roses. Furthermore, just as food properly taken into us, though not living in itself, is transformed by our own spirit into the form of our life; so too our bodies, when properly adapted to the **Worldly Body** and **Spirit**—namely through worldly things and through our...

->### ON OBTAINING LIFE FROM THE HEAVENS<-

...they carry. These things must be applied both externally and taken internally according to one’s strength; especially on the day and in the hour of the **Sun**, and while the **Sun** is reigning in the figure of the heavens <note>In astrology, the "figure of the heavens" refers to a horoscope or chart; the Sun is "reigning" when it is in a powerful position, such as the Ascendant or Midheaven.</note>. 

Indeed, **Solar** things <note>Things belonging to the Sun, or "Solar" things, were believed to share a spiritual sympathy with the Sun's power.</note> include all those small stones and flowers called **heliotropes** <term>heliotropes</term> <note>original: *elitropia*; literally "sun-turners." In the Renaissance, this referred to both the plant (like the sunflower or turnsole) and the gemstone (bloodstone), both believed to be governed by the Sun.</note> because they turn toward the sun. Likewise gold, **orpiment** <term>orpiment</term> <note>original: *auripigmentum*; a golden-yellow mineral (arsenic sulfide) used as a pigment.</note>, golden colors, chrysolite, **carbuncle** <note>A deep red gemstone, usually a garnet or ruby, thought to glow like a burning coal.</note>, myrrh, frankincense, musk, ambergris, balsam, yellow honey, aromatic reed, saffron, spikenard, cinnamon, aloe-wood, and other aromatics. Among animals: the ram, the hawk, the cock, the swan, the lion, the scarab beetle, and the crocodile. Also, men who are blonde, curly-haired, often bald, and high-spirited. 

These aforementioned things can be adapted for use partly as food, partly as ointments and **fumigations** <note>Fumigation involves burning aromatic substances to create a medicinal or ritual smoke.</note>, and partly for general utility. These things should be sensed and thought about frequently, and above all, they should be loved. And one must seek out as much light as possible. 

If you suspect that the stomach is deprived of the liver's warmth <note>The liver was traditionally viewed as the source of the body's metabolic "cooking" heat; if the stomach lacked this heat, digestion would fail.</note>, draw the power of the liver to the stomach, both by rubbing and by warm compresses made of things that agree with the liver: through chicory, endive, **spodio** <term>spodio</term> <note>original: *spodium*; a medicinal substance often made from burnt ivory or metallic oxides, used to balance heat.</note>, agrimony, liverwort, and livers themselves. 

Similarly, so that your body is not deprived of **Jupiter**, move your body on the day and in the hour and under the reign of **Jupiter**; and in the meantime, use things belonging to Jupiter: silver, hyacinth <note>A blue or violet gemstone, likely a sapphire or zircon, not the flower.</note>, topaz, coral, crystal, beryl, *spodio*, sapphire, and green and airy colors. Use wine, sugar, white honey; and occupy yourself very much with thoughts and emotions. Use **Jovial** things—that is, excellent things: horses, religious and law-giving people, and being among men of this kind, and those of a **sanguine** temperament <note>original: *sanguineos*; the sanguine temperament was associated with Jupiter, describing people who were social, cheerful, and physically robust.</note>.

### Chapter II

**Neither**, indeed, should anyone doubt that we and all things that are around us can, through certain preparations, claim for themselves celestial things. For these things were made by the heavens and are continually ruled by them; and from there, they were primarily prepared to receive those influences. And what is greatest of all is that the world is more a single living being in itself than any other animal, if indeed it is the most perfect animal <note>This refers to the *Anima Mundi* or World Soul. Ficino argues that if the universe is one giant body, the planets are like its "organs," and we are like its "cells"—therefore, what happens in the "organs" naturally affects the "cells."</note>. Therefore, just as in us the quality and movement of any member—especially a principal one <note>The "principal members" in a human are the heart, brain, and liver.</note>—pertains to the others, so the acts of the principal members in the world move all things; and the lower members easily receive from the higher ones, which are ready to give of their own accord. For the more powerful a cause is, the more ready it is to act; therefore, it is all the more inclined to give. A small preparation for us, then, <margin><unclear>dist...</unclear></margin> <margin><unclear>ane</unclear></margin> <margin><unclear>q n...</unclear></margin> <margin><unclear>tate</unclear></margin> <margin><unclear>ast...</unclear></margin> <margin><unclear>ra...</unclear></margin> <margin><unclear>till...</unclear></margin> <margin><unclear>cui...</unclear></margin> <margin><unclear>sect...</unclear></margin> <margin><unclear>ria</unclear></margin> <margin><unclear>qu...</unclear></margin> <margin><unclear>nic...</unclear></margin> <margin><unclear>rat...</unclear></margin> <margin><unclear>uia</unclear></margin> <margin><unclear>q...</unclear></margin> <margin><unclear>d...</unclear></margin> <margin><unclear>q...</unclear></margin> <margin><unclear>es</unclear></margin> <margin><unclear>re</unclear></margin> <margin><unclear>M...</unclear></margin> <margin><unclear>q...</unclear></margin> <margin><unclear>S...</unclear></margin> <margin><unclear>n...</unclear></margin> <margin><unclear>a...</unclear></margin>

<meta>This page continues Chapter II of Book III, "On Obtaining Life from the Heavens." Ficino is explaining how humans, though part of the World Soul, must consciously align themselves with specific planetary influences to receive their benefits.</meta>

### ON THE CELESTIAL LIFE

¶ ...further applied, is sufficient for receiving celestial gifts: provided that each person accommodates himself especially to that [star] to which he is particularly subject <note>Ficino refers to one's "ruling planet," determined by the position of the stars at birth.</note>. 

¶ But before considering the specific property of each of us, we must consider the property of the human species as a whole. The Arab astrologers <note>This likely refers to influential figures like Albumasar or Al-Kindi, whose works on planetary influences were foundational to Renaissance magic.</note> agreed that this species is **Solar** <note>original: *solarem*; pertaining to the Sun. Ficino views the Sun as the source of vital heat and clear intellect.</note>. I conjecture this to be true from man's erect and beautiful stature; from his subtle humors; from the clarity of his spirit and the sharpness of his imagination; and from his pursuit of truth and glory. 

¶ Furthermore, I add to this a **Mercurial** property: because of the vigorous movement of a versatile wit. And because the human race is born naked, unarmed, and in need of all things, it acquires all these things for itself by its own industry; which is the specific trait of **Mercury** <note>Mercury is the god of craft, trade, and cleverness, associated with the "industry" required to overcome human physical vulnerability.</note>. 

¶ I also add a **Jovial** <note>original: *Iouialem*; pertaining to Jupiter.</note> property: because of the temperate complexion of the body and the existence of laws. And because in the second month [of gestation], in which **Jupiter** rules, we receive life; and we are born in the ninth month, when he again receives dominion. 

¶ Thus, the human species can at last claim for itself even more ample gifts from these three planets if, through **Solar**, **Mercurial**, and **Jovial** things, it accommodates itself to them more and more every day. 

¶ But what of the others? **Saturn** does not easily signify the common quality and lot of the human race; but rather a man set apart from others: either divine or brutish, blessed or weighed down by extreme misery <note>Saturn was known as the planet of extremes, ruling over both the highest contemplative philosophers and the most wretched outcasts.</note>. **Mars**, the **Moon**, and **Venus** signify the affec—

<margin>i.iiii.</margin>

<summary>Ficino argues that the human species is naturally aligned with the Sun, Mercury, and Jupiter, citing physical traits, human industry, and the timing of birth. He distinguishes Saturn as a planet of extremes that sets individuals apart from the common lot of humanity.</summary>

### ON THE CELESTIAL LIFE

...spirit and actions are equally common to humans and other animals. Therefore, let us return to the **Sun**, **Jupiter**, and **Mercury**. We have already mentioned several things pertaining to the Sun and Jupiter; however, I do not know how we overlooked things pertaining to **Mercury** <note>original: *Mercurialia*</note>. They are as follows: Tin <note>While tin is usually associated with Jupiter, Ficino often groups materials based on complex shared qualities</note>, silver—and especially quicksilver <note>original: *argentum vivum*; literally "living silver," known today as the element mercury</note>—silver marcasite, the agate stone, glass, porphyry, and things that mix saffron-yellow with green, such as emerald and lac <note>A resinous secretion of insects used as a dye or varnish</note>. Among animals, those that are wise and clever as well as vigorous: monkeys and dogs. Among men: the eloquent, the sharp, and the versatile; those with long faces and lean hands.

¶ Those things pertaining to a particular planet must be sought out and put to use while that planet is ruling (as we have said) on its day and in its hour, if possible. They should also be used when the planet is in its own house <note>the zodiac sign where the planet resides</note>, or its exaltation <note>the sign where the planet's power is most intensified</note>, or at least in its triplicity <note>one of the four sets of three zodiac signs belonging to the same element</note>, its bound <note>original: *termino*; a specific degree-range within a sign</note>, and in an angle of the sky <note>the four most powerful houses of a chart: the ascendant, descendant, midheaven, and imum coeli</note>. It should be moving in direct motion <note>not appearing to move backward in "retrograde"</note>, outside of combustion <note>original: *extra combustionem*; when a planet is too close to the Sun's "burning" light to be effective</note>, and more often in the east if it is higher than the Sun. <margin>AUS, but, and</margin> Likewise, when it is at its apogee <note>original: *auge*; the point in a planet's orbit farthest from Earth, associated with its highest or purest influence</note> and is aspected by the Moon. <margin>del, ter, ni, &</margin>

¶ If anyone asks for a benefit from the **Moon** herself or from **Venus**, they must observe similar times. From **Venus**, benefits come through her animals which we mentioned, and through carnelian, sapphire, lapis lazuli, brass <note>original: *æs croceum*; literally "saffron-colored bronze"</note> and copper <note>original: *rubeum*; literally "red [bronze]"</note>, coral, and all beautiful and varied or green colors, flowers, musical harmonies, sweet scents, and flavors. <margin>who, un, red</margin> From the **Moon**, benefits come through white, moist, and green things; through silver, crystal, pearls <note>original: *uniones*; large, single pearls</note>, and silver marcasite. <margin>Se, pl, rit, de</margin> Since, however, **Saturn**... <margin>a, p, q, d, st, ti, n, p, i, b, c</margin>

<meta>This page continues from the previous discussion on the timing of planetary influences. The text transitions from the beneficial planets (Sun, Jupiter, Mercury) to the more difficult influences of Saturn and Mars. The "nus" at the start completes the name "Saturnus" (Saturn).</meta>

-># ON THE CELESTIAL LIFE<-

**Saturn** <note>original: *Saturnus*</note> indeed rules over stability and perseverance; **Mars**, however, rules over the efficacy of motion. We are sometimes compelled to demand protection from them for their sake as well, provided that the proper times are observed in a similar way. From **Saturn** indeed, we seek protection through certain materials that are in some way earthy, dark, and leaden, such as dark jasper, the magnet <note>the lodestone, valued for its attractive power</note>, the camone <note>likely a reference to a variety of agate or a specific "stony" mineral</note> and chalcedony, and in some part through gold and golden marcasite <note>a brittle, metallic mineral often called "fool's gold," though here associated with Saturn due to its hidden qualities</note>. From **Mars**, however, we seek it through fiery and red things: red brass <note>copper</note>, all sulfurous things, iron, and the bloodstone <note>original: *lapidem sanguineum*; hematite, which was believed to stop bleeding</note>. 

And do not doubt that **Saturn** has some share in gold. For it is thought to possess it because of its weight. Furthermore, gold, being similar to the **Sun**, is present in all metals just as the **Sun** is present among all the planets and stars. Now, if anyone should argue that **Saturn** and **Mars** are harmful by nature—though I myself would never believe this—nevertheless, they must also be used, just as physicians sometimes use poisons. Ptolemy proves this in his *Centiloquium* <note>A collection of one hundred astrological aphorisms traditionally attributed to the astronomer Claudius Ptolemy</note>. 

Therefore, the power of **Saturn**, when taken cautiously, will sometimes be beneficial, just as with physicians who use things that are astringent and binding, or even those that stupefy, such as opium and mandrake. The power of **Mars** is likewise useful, like euphorbium <note>a caustic, medicinal resin from the spurge plant</note> and hellebore. Indeed, the Brahman Magi <note>Indian sages whom Ficino views as part of an ancient lineage of wisdom</note> and the Pythagoreans seem to have been most cautious in this matter. Since they, on account of their diligent study of philosophy, were under the tyranny <note>original: *tyr...*; likely *tyrannidem*</note> of **Saturn**...

<summary>Ficino explains how even "malefic" planets like Saturn and Mars can be utilized for their unique properties—stability and motion—provided they are used with the caution a physician uses when prescribing poison.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discussion on how human activities and mental states align us with specific planetary influences. While the previous page ended by mentioning the power of Saturn, this page begins a broader rule on how our "spirit"—the intermediary between mind and body—acts as a lightning rod for celestial forces.</meta>

Everywhere, however, remember that through the affection and pursuit of the mind, and through the quality of the spirit itself <note>original: *spiritus*; in Neoplatonic medicine, this is a subtle vapor that connects the physical body to the immaterial soul</note>, we are very easily and suddenly exposed to the planets that signify that same affection, pursuit, and quality. 

Therefore, through separation from human affairs: through leisure, solitude, and steadfastness; through theology and the more secret philosophy <note>likely referring to esoteric or Hermetic studies</note>, superstition <note>original: *superstitionem*; here used to mean intense religious devotion or ritual practice</note>, magic, and agriculture; and through sorrow, we are subjected to **Saturn**. 

Through civic and ambitious business, through natural and common philosophy, through civil religion, and through laws, we are subjected to **Jupiter**. 

To **Mars**, through anger and contests. 

To the **Sun** and **Mercury**, through the study of eloquence, and songs, and truth, and glory, and skill. 

To **Venus**, through joy, music, and festivities. 

To the **Moon**, through a way of life similar to that of plants <note>referring to the "vegetative soul" which governs basic biological functions like growth and nutrition</note>. 

But keep this distinction in mind: the more public and grand exercise of talent belongs to the **Sun**; but the private kind, devoted to craftsmanship, belongs rather to **Mercury**. Furthermore, grave music belongs to **Jupiter** and the **Sun**; light music to **Venus**; and middle music to **Mercury**.

A similar reasoning applies to the fixed stars. This indeed is the general rule for the human species. 

But the specific rule for each individual would be to investigate which star promised what good to whom in their birth chart <note>original: *genitura*; the specific arrangement of stars at the moment of an individual's birth</note>, and to seek favor from that star rather than another. And one should not expect just any gift from every star, nor what belongs to others, but that which is its own property—unless perhaps many common things are received from the **Sun**...

<meta>This page continues the discussion of how human activities align with planetary influences. ### ON LIFE FROM THE HEAVENS.

...to all parts, as if by a certain common leader of the heavens, and similarly by Jupiter; then likewise all worldly things are influenced by the soul and the spirit of the world. That the world is animated <note>original: *animatum*; meaning "possessing a soul." Ficino argues the universe is a living organism, not a dead machine.</note>, just like any animal but much more effectively, is proven not only by Platonic reasoning but also by the testimony of Arabian astrologers <note>likely referring to scholars like Al-Kindi or Albumasar, whose works on "astray rays" influenced Renaissance magic</note>. They also demonstrate that through a certain application of our own spirit to the spirit of the world—by means of natural art and sacred devotion—celestial benefits are drawn into our soul and body. 

This occurs through our spirit acting as a mediator within us, which is then strengthened by the spirit of the world; likewise, the rays of the stars act beneficially upon our spirit (which is similar in nature to those rays), and our spirit then adapts itself to these celestial influences.

Between the soul of the world and its body, there is clearly its spirit, in whose power the four elements reside. We, in turn, are able to draw this in through our own spirit. 

**Chapter III.**

**I**ndeed, the body of the world—as is apparent from its motion and generation—is a single unit everywhere; philosophers prove this by the fact that it generates living things from itself in all places. Therefore, it lives by means of a soul that is present everywhere within it and perfectly suited to it. Therefore, between the world’s tangible and partly perishable body and its soul—the nature of which is vastly different from such a body—there exists spirit everywhere. This is just as it is between the soul and the body within us, provided that life is always communicated from the soul to the body everywhere.

<summary>Ficino explains that the universe is a living organism connected by a 'World Spirit' (spiritus mundi). He argues that humans can draw down celestial benefits by aligning their own internal spirit with this universal medium.</summary>
<keywords>spiritus mundi, world soul, Marsilio Ficino, astrology, Neoplatonism, celestial influence, Arabian astrology, four elements</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the technical explanation of the "World Spirit" (spiritus mundi). The previous page established the world as a living organism; here, the author explains the necessity of a "middle" substance (spirit) that allows the divine soul to interact with the heavy, physical matter of the body.</meta>

...coarser <note>Continuing from the previous page: "coarser matter."</note>. For such a spirit is necessarily required as a medium: by which the divine soul may be present in the **coarser body**, and may bestow life upon it completely. Every body, however, which is easily perceptible to you and adapted to your senses, is coarser and far degenerated from the most divine soul. There is need, therefore, for the assistance of a more excellent body, which is almost as if it were not a body at all. Accordingly, we know that all living things—both **plants** and **animals**—live and generate by means of a certain spirit similar to this. And among the elements, that which is most spiritual <note>Likely referring to the element of Fire or Air.</note> generates most swiftly and is perpetually moved as if it were alive. 

But you might ask meanwhile: if the elements and living creatures generate something similar to themselves by their own spirit, why do **stones** and **metals** not generate, since they are intermediate between elements and living creatures? The reason, clearly, is that the spirit in them is confined by a coarser matter. If this spirit were ever properly separated and kept secret, it would be able to generate something like itself as a seminal virtue. This is possible if it were only applied to some matter of the same kind, just as diligent <term>physicists</term> <note>In this context, "physicists" refers to alchemists or natural philosophers who study the physical properties of matter.</note> separate a certain spirit from **gold** by a kind of sublimation over fire; if they apply the power of this spirit to metals, they will produce gold. Such a spirit, properly drawn from gold or from another substance and preserved, the **Arabian astrologers** call the <term>elixir</term> <note>original: *elixir*; derived from the Arabic *al-iksīr*, a medicinal or transformative powder.</note>. 

[blue paragraph mark] But let us return to the **spirit of the world**, through which the world generates all things; for indeed, all things generate through their own spirit. This spirit we can call both the "heaven" and the <term>fifth essence</term> <note>original: *quinta essentia*; the "quintessence" or ether, the celestial substance distinct from the four terrestrial elements.</note>. It is of such a nature in the body of the world as it is in our...

# ¶ ON LIFE FROM THE HEAVENS.

...in our own spirit. This first exception must be made: that the **World Soul** does not draw this spirit from the four elements as if from its own humors <note>In ancient medicine, humors were the four bodily fluids (blood, phlegm, black bile, yellow bile) that determined health. Ficino is saying the universe's spirit isn't a byproduct of its "fluids" like ours is.</note>, in the way our soul draws ours from our humors. On the contrary, the World Soul produces this spirit most directly (to speak in the manner of Plato or Plotinus) from its own generative power, as if swelling with it; and together with it, it produces the **stars**. Then, through this spirit, it immediately gives birth to the four elements, as if all things were contained within the power of that spirit. 

This spirit itself is a most subtle body, almost as if it were not a body at all, and yet almost already a soul. Likewise, it is almost not a soul, and yet almost already a body. Within its power, there is very little of an earthy nature, but more of a watery nature, and even more of an airy nature. Again, it contains the greatest amount of fiery and stellar nature. According to the measures of these degrees, the quantities of the stars and elements themselves have come forth. 

Indeed, this spirit flourishes everywhere in all things as the immediate author and motion of all generation; regarding which that poet <note>The Roman poet Vergil, in the *Aeneid* VI.726: *spiritus intus alit* ("the spirit nourishes from within").</note> said: **"The spirit nourishes from within."** By its very nature, it is entirely bright, warm, moist, and life-giving, having obtained these gifts from the higher gifts of the soul. The same Iarchas <note>Iarchas was a legendary Indian sage or Brahman mentioned in the biography of the philosopher Apollonius.</note> testified that **Apollonius of Tyana** <note>A 1st-century philosopher and reputed miracle-worker.</note> had drunk most deeply of this spirit, saying: "O Apollonius, no one should wonder that you have attained the science of divination, since you carry so much of the **ether** in your soul."

### ¶ Our spirit draws the spirit of the world through the rays of the Sun and Jupiter, insofar as it is itself solar and jovial. Chapter IV.

Therefore, you should strive above all else to draw this spirit into yourself. For by this medium, you will obtain certain natural benefits: both from the body of the world, from its soul, and even from the stars; and...

...of demons. For the spirit itself is the intermediary between the coarse body of the world and the Soul. Within this spirit are the stars and **demons** <note>In this context, *demons* (daemons) refers to tutelary spirits or intermediary beings in Neoplatonic philosophy, not necessarily evil entities.</note>; and it is through the spirit that they act. For whether the body of the world and worldly things proceed most directly from the **World Soul** <note>original: *anima mundi*</note> (as **Plotinus** and **Porphyry** prefer), or whether the worldly body and soul proceed most directly from God (as our own Christian thinkers and perhaps **Timaeus the Pythagorean** prefer), the world certainly lives and breathes, and we are permitted to draw in its spirit. 

Moreover, this spirit is specifically drawn in by man through his own spirit, which is by its very nature conformed to that World Spirit—especially if it is rendered even more akin to it through art; that is, if it becomes as heavenly as possible. It becomes **heavenly** <note>original: *caelestis*</note> if it is purged of "filth" and of all those things which cling to it that are unlike the heavens. These impurities, if they are present not only within the internal organs but also in the mind, on the skin, in clothing, or in one’s dwelling and the air, frequently infect the spirit. 

The spirit will finally be made heavenly if it creates its own "orbits" according to the circular motion of the mind and body; if it glows <note>original: *subrutilet* — to glow with a reddish or golden light</note> under more frequent contemplation and thought of light; and if things similar to the heavens are applied to it with that same diligence with which **Avicenna** <note>A famous Persian physician and philosopher whose work *On the Powers of the Heart* influenced medieval and Renaissance medicine.</note> treats the spirit in his book *On the Powers of the Heart*. And as we ourselves strive to treat it in our book *On Caring for the Health of Scholars*. In that work, first, the clouding vapors are separated from the spirit by using such purging medicines. Second, it is brightened by shining things. Third, it is cultivated so that...

...and if things similar to the heavens are applied to it with that **subtlety** <note>original: *tenuitas* — referring to the refinement or "thinness" of the spirit's substance, allowing it to be more receptive to celestial influences.</note> and, at the same time, are strengthened. 

# ON LIFE FROM THE HEAVENS
<note>original: *DE VITA CAELITUS* — This is the title of the third book of Marsilio Ficino’s influential 1489 treatise *Three Books on Life* (*De vita libri tres*).</note>

Finally, the spirit will become **heavenly** <note>original: *caelestis*</note> to the highest degree—as much as current reason dictates—if the rays and the **influence** <note>original: *influxus* — a technical astrological term for the "flowing in" of power from the stars to the terrestrial world.</note> of the sun, which rules among the celestial bodies, are applied to it. And so, from this spirit—acting as an intermediary within us—celestial benefits, primarily those implanted in the spirit itself, will overflow into both our body and our soul. All celestial benefits, I say. For in the sun, all things are contained.

Moreover, the sun will specifically contribute to making the spirit **solar** <note>original: *solarem* — sharing the qualities of the sun, such as vital heat, clarity, and life-giving power.</note> when it is in the signs of **Aries or Leo** <note>The sun is "exalted" in Aries and "rules" Leo, making its influence strongest in these signs.</note>, while the moon beholds it—especially in Leo. In that position, the sun enlivens our spirit to such an extent that it fortifies it to repel the poison of an **epidemic** <note>original: *epidimie* — referring to the plague or other widespread contagious diseases common in the Renaissance.</note>. This is clearly seen in Babylon and Egypt, and in regions pertaining to Leo; there, when the sun enters Leo, it settles the epidemic solely by the means we have described. Therefore, at that time, you should also compose exquisite **solar things** <note>These would include medicines, foods, scents, or talismans associated with the sun’s planetary nature.</note> everywhere. Then begin to use these solar things, though with the caution that you diligently avoid dehydration during the heat.

However, the solar spirit cannot easily emerge unless it is very abundant. For to the sun belongs, above all, **amplitude** <note>original: *amplitudo* — a sense of greatness, expansion, or abundance.</note>. Indeed, the spirit will become most abundant through the diligence of warming the heart with **cordials** <note>original: *cordialibus* — substances or medicines meant to stimulate and support the heart, the seat of the vital spirit.</note> both internally and externally, and also through a diet of foods that are subtle, yet nourish greatly, easily, and healthfully. Also necessary are frequent and light movement, timely rest, and air that is thin, serene, and removed from both heat and frost—and, above all, a **joyful mind**.

<meta>This page continues Marsilio Ficino’s instructions on cultivating a "solar" spirit. The previous page ended with a discussion on how the sun’s influence can help the human spirit repel the poison of epidemics.</meta>

Again, the spirit will not be solar unless it is hot, **subtle** <note>original: *subtilis* — meaning refined or thin in substance, allowing it to move quickly and receive celestial influence.</note>, and clear. You will make it subtle and clear if you avoid things that are sad, thick, and dark. Use bright and joyful things both within and without. You shall receive a mixture of light by day and by night. You must expel filth, idleness, and lethargy. Above all, you shall avoid darkness.

When you are leading the spirit toward the natural heat of the sun, take care that you do not lead it to the **third degree of heat and dryness** <note>In the medical tradition of the time, qualities like heat were measured in four degrees. The "third degree" is intense and potentially harmful, associated with scorching or inflammation.</note>. For the sun’s heat itself does not naturally dry things out; otherwise, the sun would not be the lord of life and generation, and the author of growth. However, it happens that the sun's rays dry things when they are trapped within the hollows of dry matter. Therefore, you shall apply a **subtle moisture** <note>original: *humorem... subtilem* — a balanced fluid that keeps the heat from becoming destructive.</note> to the heat—the kind of moisture that is solar, and especially **Jovial** <note>original: *iouis* — pertaining to the planet Jupiter, which was considered "temperate" and life-sustaining.</note>—and you shall preserve it in the spirit by using things of this nature. This is essential if you intend to make the spirit solar, lest you accidentally lead it toward a **Martian** <note>original: *martium* — relating to Mars, the planet of aggression, excessive heat, and strife.</note> nature rather than a solar one.

[red paragraph mark] They say that Mars is indeed similar to the sun in a few obvious ways—though not in ways that are particularly excellent—and that he is, at the same time, an enemy. But we know that **Jupiter** is most similar to the sun in his most excellent gifts (though they are more hidden) and is most friendly to it. Hence **Ptolemy** <note>Claudius Ptolemy, the 2nd-century astronomer and astrologer whose works formed the basis of Renaissance celestial science.</note>, where he discusses **harmony** <note>original: *consonantia* — astrological resonance or "aspects" between planets that create a favorable influence.</note>, says that Jupiter harmonizes most perfectly with the sun above all others, just as Venus does with the moon. All astrologers attribute a universal **beneficence** <note>The quality of being "benefic" or naturally helpful and life-giving in an astrological sense.</note> to the sun, just as they do to Jupiter. Although the sun acts more effectively in these matters, Jupiter acts under the power of the sun. In both, heat flourishes and overcomes moisture. But in Jupiter, heat overcomes it moderately; in the sun, it overcomes...

--- Page 147 ---
<meta>This page continues the instructions for refining the human spirit using celestial influences. While the previous page focused on avoiding the harsh heat of Mars by using "Jovial" (Jupiter-like) moisture, this page explains how to synchronize the solar and Jovial influences through astrological timing.</meta>

# ON LIFE FROM THE HEAVENS

Similarly: beneficent in both ways. Since they harmonize so much, you can easily make the spirit **solar** <note>original: *solarem* — associated with the Sun's clarity, vital heat, and power.</note> and **Jovial** <note>original: *Iovium* — associated with the planet Jupiter, which was considered "temperate" and the "greater benefic" in astrology.</note> at the same time; and you can properly mix solar and Jovial things together. 

This is especially effective if you combine these things with each other and apply them to the spirit when Jupiter regards the Sun with a **trine** <note>original: *trino* — an astrological aspect where planets are 120 degrees apart, considered the most harmonious and beneficial alignment.</note> aspect, or a **sextile** <note>original: *sextili* — an aspect of 60 degrees, considered helpful and productive.</note>, or at least when the Moon proceeds from the aspect of one to the aspect of the other. It is most effective when the Moon proceeds from an aspect with the Sun toward a **conjunction** <note>original: *coitum* — literally a "coming together," referring to when two celestial bodies meet at the same point in the zodiac.</note> with Jupiter. 

Separately, however, you will make the spirit properly solar or Jovial when you observe the aspect of the Moon toward the Sun or toward Jupiter. For just as if you have attained the nature of the one, you will soon easily attain the nature of the other. 

Understand a **sextile aspect**, indeed, to be when two planets are distant from each other by the space of two zodiac signs. But a **trine** occurs when four <note>The text cuts off here; a trine aspect occurs when planets are four zodiac signs (120 degrees) apart.</note>

<summary>Ficino explains how to synthesize the influences of the Sun and Jupiter to benefit the human spirit, emphasizing the importance of astrological timing and the Moon's movements. He begins a technical definition of astrological aspects like the sextile and trine.</summary>

<keywords>Marsilio Ficino, medical astrology, Sun, Jupiter, Moon, trine, sextile, conjunction, celestial spirit, planetary aspects</keywords>

# ON LIFE FROM THE HEAVENS

Venus is most friendly to Jupiter, just as Jupiter is to the Sun. They are like the **three Graces** <note>In Greek mythology, the Charites (Graces) represent charm, beauty, and creativity. Ficino associates them with the three "benefic" planets: the Sun, Jupiter, and Venus.</note> in harmony and joined with one another. From these three Graces of heaven, and from stars of the same nature, astrologers both hope for and diligently seek favors. They believe these favors are transmitted through Mercury and the Moon as if by messengers, and they take great care in this; it happens easily and commonly through the Moon. 

Indeed, they consider the Moon to be more fortunate when joined in **conjunction** <note>original: *coniunctam* — when two celestial bodies appear at the same point in the zodiac.</note> with Jupiter or Venus than if she regards them through a **sextile** or **trine** aspect. Nevertheless, if she looks through a trine and is also **received** <note>original: *suscipiatur* — "Reception" occurs in astrology when a planet is in a sign ruled by the planet it is currently looking at, creating a hospitable bond between them.</note> by them, they consider it as if she were in conjunction. The same applies if she is seen by the Sun and "received" at the same time. 

If we, however, wished to run through all the powers and effects of these three and similar stars individually, we would be undertaking a long work: difficult to investigate, and most difficult to observe in practice. If we turn ourselves specifically to Venus, we do not easily possess the Sun’s influence. If we turn specifically to the Sun, we do not easily possess Venus. 

Therefore, so that we may embrace these three Graces together in one, we shall finally take refuge in **Jupiter**. He is, by nature and effect, a middle point between the Sun and Venus. He is perfectly temperate in quality, and in his own way, he delivers whatever is hoped for from either Venus or the Sun. He is more magnificent and honorable than Venus, more temperate than the Sun, and in all things most compatible with human nature. <margin>dignity</margin>

We shall therefore use **Jovial** <note>Things pertaining to the planet Jupiter, associated with health, law, and vital spirits.</note> things when both Jupiter himself and the Moon are in their **dignity**... <note>original: *dignitatem* — a planet's "dignity" refers to it being in a position of strength within the zodiac, such as its own "house."</note>
